ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u8	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Maybe two sentences on the origins of this bill, because it's important. As we all know, the financial crisis that started in 2007 has shown that there are no effective instruments at both EU and national level to deal with bankrupt or failing financial institutions. The need to have an appropriate legal framework for the compulsory restructuring of financial institutions to enable the rapid restructuring of the insolvency-threatening entity, regardless of its size and market links, led to work in the European forum which resulted in the adoption by the European Parliament of 12 June 2014 of a Directive published in the Official Journal of the European Union as Directive 2014/59/EU. This Directive requires Member States to implement in their national legal systems the powers to conduct compulsory restructuring proceedings by means of instruments: primarily the acquisition of a company, then bridge institutions, the separation of assets or finally the redemption or conversion of debts. This directive must, of course, be implemented in the legal systems of the Member States. As a result, work was also undertaken in Poland to prepare an appropriate bill. This work continued during the previous parliamentary term. They were run by the previous government. It lasted for quite a long time, took about two years. Finally, it turned out that, despite a long period of preparation, a project that had gone to Parliament requires quite profound changes. The project was large, extensive, containing three hundred and dozen articles of very complex matter. Nevertheless, the Committee on Public Finance and the Subcommittee on Financial Institutions led by Mr Szewczak in a very short period of time worked this project despite, as I say, the need to make far-reaching changes to this project. This need resulted in more than 200 amendments to this project. Of course, one might ask why this project was so underdeveloped despite such a long period of preparation. However, this is already a question which I hope will be answered by the representatives of the party that previously ruled, the Civic Platform. As a result of this work, we have today a project which has been submitted to the High House as a report by the Committee on Public Finance. I will no longer discuss the details of this project, because Mr Cymanski did this to me. I would like to ask the High House to accept this proposal despite some discussions that have not yet been closed. I think that they will continue, also in the Senate forum, which, following the Seym, will examine this law. I think that there may still be a need for some detail, there will still be some changes in this project, which we see at this point in time, at this stage. However, we are required sometimes, because we are also threatened with financial consequences in the form of penalties by the European Commission if we continue this project. As I say, we did not have this temporary comfort in the Committee on Public Finance to examine this law in the way it certainly deserves, and to address all aspects that involve the proposed provisions, but as far as we could, in fact, in a relatively short time we leaned on this law. ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u16	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The aim of this law was indeed to bring Polish legislation into line with European Union legislation, since it implements two EU directives to the Polish legal system. The first changes the rules for guaranteeing the solvency of deposits in Poland by the Bank's Guarantee Fund and aims to increase the safety of savings customers, while the second sets out the framework and principles for BFG's remedial and forced restructuring activities in financial institutions in Poland. Unfortunately, as already mentioned here, during the work on this law, the care of the PiS about the interests of the near Law and Justice environment of the SKOKs significantly distorted the original purpose of this law, especially as regards the privileged position of the SKOKs. During the committee's work, the most discussions really focused on whether the new law on BFG should include cooperative savings and credit banks or not. One of the options very strongly pushed with the participation of the Head of the sub-committee of Mr Szewczak over the years associated with the SKOKs assumed a significant weakening of the control of the Financial Supervision Commission over this group of institutions. Why is this topic so important? Let us remind you that three years ago, deposits in SKOKs were not covered by the KNF control and the payment of guaranteed deposits. During this time, in the event of the collapse of one of the cashiers, customers would simply remain penniless - and that must be said explicitly. Fortunately, in 2013, when the Parliamentary Club was opposed, SKOK Law and Justice were included in the supervision of KNF and customer deposits were covered by guarantees. Politicians and those defending the SKOKs at that time probably did not want their inconsistencies and the spectrum of insolvency to be publicly discovered. When KNF took charge of SKOK's supervision, he threw out most of the cashier managers, accusing them of incompetence and errors in the functioning of these institutions. In 2013 alone, 40 out of 55 banks introduced resolution programmes and two commission boards. Two SKOK-i collapsed in 2014 - the Community and Volomin have proved completely insolvent. Customers received their deposits back only and only because in 2013 SKOK-i were covered by the Bank Guarantee Fund, i.e. if Parliament listened to the Law and Justice statements, customers, as in the Amber Gold event, would simply be without money. [[There were only six times as many here.]] The liquidation and acquisitions by banks were held by SKOK-i, in which the money - PLN 5.5 billion - held close to 450 thousand. Members. According to the financial report of the National Bank of Poland, the situation of the SKOK sector remains difficult and requires the strengthening of repair and restructuring processes, and Law and Justice again opposes it. At the end of April this year, 43 SKOKs were active, including two with the participation of the commissary board. 36 cashiers are subject to the obligation to develop and implement corrective programmes. So far, KNF has accepted corrective programmes in only 10 of them. To make matters worse, only five of the programmes are implemented in line with the assumptions made. KNF continues to conduct administrative proceedings for the establishment of a commission board in as many as 17 cash registers, including the National Cashier. As we read in the opinion of the KNF presented on the occasion of this law, due to the practical exhaustion of the stabilisation fund's resources to carry out remedial actions in the SKOK sector, the KNF examines the possibility of further remedial actions involving banks. In this situation, the amendments tabled by the representative of the National Casa, which I will remind you, were to exclude the SKOKs from this law and put them in a privileged position, we regard them as impudence and arrogance. And we call on the representatives of Law and Justice to take account of the tragic situation in the sector at a later stage in the work on the law. Let us remember that, in fact, the funds raised in the guarantee fund are the money that all bank and financial institutions customers are throwing themselves into. The costs of inability and irresponsibleness of some of these financial institutions are simply borne by Poles. [[♪ The bell ♪]] It is also necessary to reconsider the possibility that BFG's contributions constitute a cost for financial institutions to obtain revenue. The exclusion of these charges from costs will make Poles simply pay more.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u28	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Polish People's Party Parliamentary Club, I have the honour to present my position on the committee's report on the government print project No 215. Report from the committee - print No 502. At the beginning of my speech, I will say a few words about the main objectives and principles of the Banking Guarantee Fund, because it is an important project for financial security reasons. The project is the implementation of two EU Directives: the BRRD on Recovery and Resolution for Credit Institutions and Investment Companies and the DGSD on Deposit Guarantee Schemes. The tasks arising from these directives will be carried out in the Polish system through the Bank Guarantee Fund. It is the BFG that will take action to promote the stability of the national financial system, in particular by ensuring the functioning of the mandatory deposit guarantee scheme and the conduct of compulsory restructuring proceedings. The project was the subject of an analysis of the sub-committee on financial institutions, as well as the Committee on Public Finance. The project itself is a very comprehensive document, extended both in its original form and in its submission to the committee. Many of the amendments resulting from the evaluation of the Legislative Office as well as comments submitted by financial institutions: the National Bank of Poland, the Financial Supervision Commission and the Association of Polish Banks were considered during the project. A very large group of amendments was initiated by the SKOK National Cashier. They were included in the draft already at the stage of the subcommittee's work with the approval of some of the members of the subcommittee and the approval of the government, and some of these amendments, as a variant of the draft, were included in the support of the parliamentarians of Law and Justice at the committee meeting. Unfortunately, today's project is a much worse version than the government's submission. The fact of diversity, for example, raises huge concerns. the amount of contributions paid by banks and SKOKs in the draft version submitted by the committee. In the course of the work of the subcommittee, far-reaching preferences were applied to the cash registers, i.e. the SKOKs. And so: the target level of the deposit guarantee scheme in the original government version of the project was 2,8% the amounts guaranteed in cash registers, at the same value in banks and was reduced to up to 1% in cash registers and not much, because up to 2.6% at the banks. Similarly, and even more disparately, there is a situation as regards contributions to compulsory restructuring. For banks, the target for funding compulsory restructuring is 1.2% of the guaranteed amount, at a rate of 1.4%. proposed in the government submission, while the cash register reduced the minimum level of funding for compulsory restructuring from 1% to 0,1% , and the target level of 1.4% up to 0.14%. Such drastic differentiation of contributions between e.g. cooperative banks and cash registers are simply unacceptable. In fact, it is not banks, and the SKOKs require greater security of funds due to the high risk of their existing activities. Only last year, over PLN 4 billion of the guarantee fund was paid out of the funds - collected by the banks - due to the poor management of public funds in the SKOKs. Unfortunately, the comments made by the Financial Supervision Commission should also be raised. Its supervision is severely restricted, especially with regard to the National Money. Similar reservations are expressed by the National Bank of Poland. He claims that there has been interference with the Act on the National Bank of Poland, that the tasks of the Central Bank have been changed and that the proposed amendments have not been the subject of consultation with the European Central Bank. The only argument in the motivation of this state of affairs, the preference for cash registers, allegedly follows that EU directives do not require credit unions to be covered by deposit guarantees and resolution schemes provided for in those Directives. In conclusion, the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party [[♪ The bell ♪]] does not support the draft law as it stands. The numerous preferences applied to SKOKs, the enigmatic justification that this is the result of the EU directive - all of this is inconclusive, and in fact aims to continue to shift the cost of guaranteeing deposits and the cost of forced restructuring of banks into the banking sector, including cooperative banks. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[Bravo!]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u54	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Education, Science and Youth on the draft law on amending the Law on Research Institutes. This report is contained in print No 483, and the draft law to which it relates is published in print No 292. High Izbo! These regulations concern state research institutes. There are about 115 research institutes in Poland. On the other hand, the state is 17. They play a special role. These are institutes that must carry out activities commissioned by the Minister. Therefore, the relationship and cooperation between these institutes and the supervising minister must, by nature, be very coherent. The current provisions of the Act prevent effective supervision of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs over them. This is particularly dangerous for institutes performing tasks which are particularly important for the planning and implementation of state policy. This draft law concerns the amendment of selected provisions of the Law on Research Institutes in order to strengthen the supervision of the Minister of Supervision, in particular as regards: the appointment of a Director from among candidates selected through a competition, matters relating to the day-to-day management of the Research Institute in the event of a failure to appoint a Director as a result of a competition, the possibility of repealing resolutions of the Scientific Council which are not in conformity with the laws and statutes and the extension of the grounds for appeal of the Director of the Research Institute. The draft law assumes that the Supervisory Minister appoints one person from among the candidates submitted by the competition committee to the Director of the Research Institute which meets the formal requirements. At the same time, it was foreseen that the Supervisory Minister may not appoint a Director where none of the candidates submitted by the competition committee guarantees the proper performance of the Institute's tasks. Such a solution gives the Minister greater opportunities to make the right choice of person for the most important position in the institution. In addition, the draft provides that, in the absence of appointment to the Director, any of the candidates submitted by the Competition Committee may be appointed by the Supervisory Minister to perform the duties of the Director not only of the former Deputy Director. It is appropriate to confer on the Minister supervising the power to appoint and dismiss the Deputy Director of the State Research Institute, since in the event of the cancellation of the Director of the Institute it is often so that the reasons for his dismissal by the Minister of Supervision also refer to his Deputy Director. Then the appointment of a Deputy Director to perform his duties could also affect the principles of the rule of law, integrity and economy that must undoubtedly be respected in all institutes, including in the activities of the State Institute. It should be borne in mind that at present the competent minister cannot revoke the Deputy Director of the Institute, only the Director of the Institute, the Director or the Head of the Institute. In practice, please, it may happen that in the event that the supervising minister cancels the Director of the Institute or does not appoint a candidate designated by the competition committee, the former Deputy Director will refuse to accept or perform the duties of Director. In such a case, the activities of the research institute may be de facto paralyzed. This is to prevent this kind of undesirable paralysis of the Institute's activities. It should be stressed that the deputy will have to meet the same strict requirements as the Institute's Director. In this way, the draft law aims to improve the quality of the management of the research institute during the transitional period of the absence of its director. The project also provides an additional possibility to dismiss the Director because the Institute has failed to perform the tasks specified in the Statute. This proposal is intended to improve the Minister's supervision of the State Research Institute. It is also foreseen that in the event of reorganisation of the Institute consisting of a change in the subject matter or scope of its operation, the Supervisory Minister may revoke the former Director and appoint a person to carry out his duties. It is also proposed that two representatives of the Minister of Supervision should be included in the selection committee for the appointment of candidates for director, not one so far. Consequently, the number of representatives of the Scientific Council is decreasing from two to one. In order to strengthen the Minister's supervision, the composition of the Scientific Council should be amended in such a way that the Institute's scientific and research and technical staff represent 50% of the board's composition and those who are not members of the Institute's staff - including 50%, while the Chairman of the Scientific Council of the State Research Institute could be chosen only from among the members of the Scientific Council appointed by the Supervisory Minister. There are also new regulations concerning the examination of the legality and statute of the Institute of resolutions of the Scientific Council. The proposed regulation is intended to ensure that the Supervisory Minister is able to effectively supervise the activities of the Institute. Ladies and gentlemen, Polish scientists and students are huge potential. It will not be fully exploited if the State - and in this case the Ministry - does not have sufficient supervision over it. The other changes proposed in the draft are of a clear and precise nature to the existing provisions. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the committee, I request the adoption of an attached draft resolution... of a bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u56	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Seym! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice regarding the parliamentary draft law amending the Law on Research Institutes, print No 292. The aim of the amendment is to strengthen the supervision of the research institute by the competent minister. It should be noted that the proposed regulations concern state research institutes. The provisions currently in force prevent effective supervision of research institutes where the Director does not perform his duties properly. This constitutes a danger for institutes performing tasks which are particularly important for the planning and implementation of state policies, such as state research institutes. The proposed law provides greater opportunities for the proper choice of the most important people at the Institute, allowing the Minister of Supervision not only to appoint and dismiss the Director of the Institute, but also its deputies. It should be noted that, under the current rules, the competent minister cannot revoke the Deputy Director of the Institute, only the Director of the Institute, the Director or the Head of the Institute may do so. The proposed solution therefore guarantees the highest quality of management. The project also provides for the addition of a catalogue of grounds for appealing the Director of the grounds for incorrect performance by the Institute of its tasks recorded in the statutes. This amendment is intended to improve the Minister's supervision of the operation of state research institutes. The solutions contained in the draft law are intended to prevent unwanted paralysis of the activities of state research institutes. The project also adds new regulations concerning the examination of legality and the statute of the Institute of resolutions of the Scientific Council. These regulations are intended to ensure that the Minister oversees the activities of the institutes. The draft also contains changes that are of a clear and precise nature to the existing provisions. Mrs. Marshal! ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u57	Thank you very much, Member. Thank you very much. I invite Mr Włodzimierz Nykiel, the Civic Platform Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u58	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Civic Platform Club a position on the draft law amending the Law on Research Institutes, print No. 483. The project, which was presented by the applicants, aims to strengthen the supervision of the Minister of Supervision and in practice completely subordinates the research institutes of the Minister. Originally, the project concerned all research institutes, and now the scope of the changes has been narrowed down to state research institutes. Of course, it is better that this is the case, but these bad solutions, which were intended to refer to all institutes, now refer to state research institutes. What are the current solutions for the bodies of institutes? The director is appointed by the minister, but the candidate for the director is presented by the competition committee. There is therefore a combination of the influence of the Institute and the decisive voice of the Minister. Deputy Director now appoints and dismisses the Director, and it is natural for the Director to choose the co-workers he will work with. Today, the Scientific Council is composed of employees of the Institute representing at least 50% the composition and the persons outside the institute represent 30% of the composition. The Scientific Council shall elect from among its members by secret ballot the Chairperson and his alternates. The project looks like this. According to the provisions of the project, the Minister appoints the Director, with a large, almost full freedom of choice - if all the provisions relating to it are put together - and the role of the competition committee is minimal, actually symbolic. The Minister shall also conclude a contract with the Director and may also revoke it. The Minister shall also appoint and conclude contracts with the Deputy Director. Up to 50% of the scientific council of the state research institute is to be constituted by persons who are not employees of the institute. According to the rules of the project, the Scientific Council elects its chairman and its alternates from among its members, so it looks beautiful, but in the case of the State Research Institute, only a member of the Council appointed by the Minister can be president. As you can see, the provisions of the project, by changing the rules, are in fact fully subject to the activities of the supervisory minister's institute. This state of affairs deserves a negative assessment for at least three reasons. Firstly, it seems that it is not possible to effectively manage research institutes at the Ministry level, secondly, scientific research, however, requires a certain degree of freedom of action for its effectiveness, the possibility of making decisions, and this is missing, and thirdly, under such conditions, the state research institute may find it difficult to organise its tasks. Against this background you can ask the question - because I presented the current regulation and proposed this regulation - whether there were precedents with us regarding similar solutions. Well, ladies and gentlemen, it is worth noting that legal solutions of a similar nature - not details, but similar nature - were included in the Act of 8 January 1951 on the creation of research institutes for the purposes of the national economy and, later, in the Law on research institutes of 17 February 1961. I think that the similarity of the proposed solutions to those laws today does not bring great glory to the authors of these solutions. Let us not appeal to such patterns. The Civic Platform Club has a negative assessment [[♪ The bell ♪]] the proposed amendments and calls for these changes to be abandoned. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u60	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! The proposed amendments to the provisions of the Law on Research Institutes aim primarily at increasing the supervisory capacity of the Minister supervising a specific state research institute. According to the project, ministers will be given a wide range of opportunities to fill management positions in state research institutes. This concerns the possibility of free choice of director, i.e. selection from among several submitted candidates, or the right of appeal on the basis of non-precise, general wording, e.g. Not providing a guarantee for the proper performance of tasks, although as we know, the candidate has the required qualifications and meets the substantive criteria. It is worth mentioning that the original project concerned all institutes, after the work of the committee it now concerns only institutes with the status of a state research institute. In addition, the Scientific Council of State Research Institutes will consist of 50% employee representatives and 50% non-employees. At present, non-Institute members account for 30% of the board members. The position of the Scientific Council of State Institutes will also be weakened because it is deprived of its ability to request the appointment of a Director. She'll only be able to apply for an appeal. Unfortunately, a staff member of an institute other than a deputy appointed to act as a director will still not have to have knowledge of foreign languages. The rapporteur said that the current rules prevent the Minister from having proper control. However, according to the NIK audit carried out in recent years, the supervisory powers so far have not been properly used. The Supreme Audit Chamber states that: The supervision of ministers over research institutes focused primarily on financial and organisational issues, to a lesser extent taking into account the effects of their scientific and implementation activities. During the audit of research institutes, ministers did not use the assessments formulated by the Committee for the Evaluation of Scientific Units. Ministers failed to carry out the obligation to control institutes, which prevented a comprehensive assessment of their activities. The under-exploiting of the research potential of institutes could have had an impact on: indeterminate directions for the development of their activities, involvement in service activities, insufficient development of young researchers resulting in a generation gap and incorrect periodic evaluations of employees. The question therefore arises as to whether an additional extension of the oversight of ministers will change anything in this regard and whether it will really improve the functioning of state research institutes. Research institutes employ as many as 42,000 full-time employees in Poland. people and their activities still do not translate into obtaining important foreign patents in numbers even similar to those in Western countries. Today's practical application to the economy is only 20% That's still a small amount of patent work. The additional problems of the current system are the lack of structural reforms, the lack of proper involvement and acquisition of young researchers, the lack of cooperation with the economy and the lack of economic impact. The exchange of staff is the only change that the proposed law introduces, and it is unlikely that it will contribute to a real improvement in the situation in this area and, above all, its impact on the real improvement in the quality of work of institutes is questionable. The Kukiz Club15 calls for a deep restructuring of research institutes, even reducing them or consolidating them, not making them even more dependent on the state authorities. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u61	Thank you very much, Member. I invite Katarzyna Lubnauer, the Modern Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u62	Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Mrs. Marshal! In the explanatory memorandum of the bill amending the Law on Research Institutes, the main objective is to strengthen the supervision exercised by the competent ministers of research institutes. The authors of the project assumed that the competence of the competent minister was insufficient and needed to be extended. It is right to discuss changes to the functioning of research institutes, but the proposed changes are unfavourable. The project indicates a lack of trust in the scientific community of these institutes. The project gives great authority to the Minister and allows for the rapid exchange of directors of selected institutes. The proposed amendments will enable each institution to quickly exchange the Director and Vice-Directors for persons designated by the Minister, strengthen his representatives in the competition committee and create the possibility to appoint an unelected person as interim director even if he does not have the qualifications required in the competition. The draft law is part of the overall human policy pursued by the Law and Justice. It is based on the belief that greater public and official control will result in a better functioning of the individual. The possible implementation of the proposed amendments will lead to a reduction in the creative and creative activities of institutes which will follow the instructions of non-specialised officials. It should be noted that, on the basis of the provisions of the Law cited above, the supervisory minister is now entitled to audit in research institutes and that, in the event of failure to comply with the audit recommendations, he has the right to dismiss the director of the Institute. However, it has no competence to repeal the resolutions of the Scientific Council of the Institute. The introduction of this possibility is contrary to the principle of freedom of research. The Modern Club wants to place an amendment on the hands of the Marshal to this bill. We believe that a number of things need to be changed, although we are in general opposed to this law as a whole. The amendments are only in case, however, this law is to apply. First of all, we want to repeal the provision allowing the Director to be dismissed in the event of a change in the scope of the Institute's activities. We want to delete the paragraph that the Minister does not appoint a Director if the competition has been conducted in violation of the law or the candidate does not meet the formal requirements. Points referring to non-compliance with the competition criteria by the candidates presented by the competition committee will be removed. The adoption of the changes proposed by the State would lead to the Minister freely deciding who will be the director, without taking into account any recommendations from the competition committee. In addition, we think that e.g. the proposed amendment to Article. 27 that the Supervisory Minister appoints and dismisses the Deputy Director is too far-reaching because this power actually takes away the director's freedom to choose the closest associates. There have been no such provisions in other such laws so far. Then there is no reason for the Chairman of the Scientific Council to be elected only from among the candidates appointed by the Minister of Supervision. There's no reason to defend this record. This is another restriction on scientific freedom and powers arising from the functioning of the institute as a research body. We are equally opposed to the Minister being able to repeal resolutions by means of an administrative decision. In conclusion, the modern club opposes official interference proposed by the PiS in the field of scientific activity. We will always defend the freedom to pursue our profession, because that is the basis for fundamental trust in our fellow countrymen. It is from their integrity, entrepreneurship, creativity, diligence and, at the same time, a certain scientific freedom that we expect results in the form of good functioning of institutes, not from the strong control powers of the Ministry. The Ministry should make greater use of these powers, which it already has, instead of introducing rules which are difficult to resist - the only objective is to manually control the capacity of staff of research institutes. Therefore, first of all, I am making amendments, second, essentially Modern is against the Law on Research Institutes at all.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u98	Dear Marshal! Mr. Messengers! Gentlemen! I have the honour to present to the High House a report on the work of the Committee on Education, Science and Youth on the draft law amending the Law on Higher Education, print Nos 429 and 503. Article 4 is replaced by the following: 255 of the Law of 27 July 2005. The Law on Higher Education states that by 30 June 2016, the names of universities should be adapted to the requirements laid down in Article. 3 laws. This article sets out the criteria which the university would be entitled to use in the university's name the word. university with adjectives, e.g. The University of Natural Sciences, Medical or Technical University, or such names as Polytechnic, Academia. This is related to the university's and its organisational units' powers to grant a doctor's degree. Thus, if the university has, it has the authority to give a doctor's degree in at least 10 disciplines, including at least two qualifications in each of the three fields of science - the first field is humanistic, legal, economic, theological, the second group is mathematical, physical, earth or technical sciences, the third is biological, medical, chemical, pharmaceutical, agricultural, veterinary sciences - then the word "university" can be used in the name of this university. We take into account the fact that the deadline for fulfilling the obligation arising from the abovementioned regulations and laws is approaching, and that some universities have failed to meet these requirements despite the high quality of education, a wide offer of teaching and unique research projects and a key role in the development of the region and their elite role. Therefore, considering that it is in their own interest and in the general interest of Polish science to deprive them of their opportunity to do this work, the committee requests that the proposed amendments be adopted, i.e. that the deadline within which the university's names may be changed, i.e. extension by 30 June 2020 of the deadline for adapting university names to the requirements set out in Article. 3 laws. I would also like to point out that the proposed bill will not have any financial implications for the budget of the state and budgets of local government units, nor is it the subject of European Union law. Thank you. [[Applause]]

The rapporteur has just presented these criteria. In view of the above, I would like to draw the attention of the High House to the fact that the proposals presented in the original version of the Act in 2005 were taken together, i.e. they refer to and relate to the need for multidisciplinaryness, but they did not include, inter alia, the proposals for obtaining criteria. the characteristics of some academic centres whose specialty, brand was built on a distinctive element, e.g. with regard to the experience of the Catholic University of Lublin or the Cardinal Stefan Wyszyński University or the University of Szczecin, which held, by analogy, 19 to 13 doctoral rights obtained, but did not meet the criterion of differentiation in three fields of science. This, of course, is a certain imperfection of this original legislative proposal, that life has revealed enough. In addition, we are at a time when the Ministry of Science is preparing new systemic solutions for the university system, so it seems reasonable to expect a complete change, and the solution we are discussing today can be considered a legitimate vacatio legis for the upcoming changes. We know that the deadline for fulfilling the obligation under the regulations and regulations mentioned above is approaching, but some universities have failed to comply with these requirements. In general, this applies to only a few universities on the national scale, yet, while appreciating and recognising the high quality of education and the high level of teaching and research, which finds its expression, among other things. In the assessments of the National Accreditation Commission, and given their important importance, which was also mentioned by the rapporteur, for the development of the region, their elite role, we consider it appropriate to take into account both objective endogenous and exogenous factors affecting this state and to support the draft law amending the deadline for enforcing the requirement referred to in Article. 3 of the Higher Education Act, from 30 June 2020 We also take into account the fact that depriving them of their chance to do this job, which is in the interest of both these institutions and the general interest of Polish science, would certainly be an irreparable loss, if we were to reduce the chances of these universities and this would certainly cause secondary problems, problems related to the more difficult competitive situation in the higher education market. This could also affect the lives of graduates of these schools in the labour market. In view of the above, we therefore support the proposal to postpone the deadline within which the name of the university may be changed, i.e. the extension by 30 June 2020 of the deadline for adapting the names of universities to the requirements set out in Article. 3 laws. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the proposed changes. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u130	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Education, Science and Youth on the work on the Parliamentary draft amendment to the Law on the revision of the Law on Higher Education and some other laws, print No 433. The project entered the Sejm on 5 April 2016 and was addressed to the first reading in the committee on 20 April 2016. The first reading of the draft law took place on 12 May this year. The committee's report is contained in print No 504. During the examination of the draft law, changes have been made which do not change its substance, but only in a more correct legislative manner create the Single Anti-Plagiarism System. The draft amendment concerns the creation of a Single Anti-Plagiarity System and the introduction of an obligation for universities to check written diploma work using this system and to extend the deadline for the implementation of the obligation for university rectors to transfer the data on diploma work to a nationwide repository, whose defence took place after 30 September 2009. The obligation to check written diploma work for the detection of plagiarism has been imposed on universities in Article. This Regulation shall enter into force on the twentieth day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union. 4 Law on Higher Education, added by law amending, according to which the university is required to check written diploma work before the diploma exam using anti-plagiarism programmes cooperating with the nationwide repository of written diploma work. The purpose of this provision was to reduce the lack of integrity in the preparation of the diploma work. With regard to the implementation of this obligation, there have been difficulties with the need to purchase the appropriate software necessary to carry out the obligation to check the diploma work. These difficulties are mainly due to the generation of significant costs on the part of universities related to the purchase and administration of an appropriate anti-plagiarism system. The current state of law has thus created a double obligation on the university side: checking the work of the anti-plagiarism system, first, while at the same time paying for their significant costs of purchasing and operating the relevant software, second. Therefore, it is highly justified to develop a Single Anti-Plagiarism System which is made available free of charge to all universities in the country, which will provide comprehensive anti-Plagiarisation services for the whole higher education system and will also preserve the principle of equal access for smaller universities, for example. in competition proceedings they did not have equal opportunities with large universities or consortia of higher education institutions. By doing so, universities will be provided with a tool from the State to fulfil the obligation imposed by the law on them. The project foresees that the creation of the Single Anti-Plagic System will be the responsibility of the Minister responsible for higher education, who will be able to commission both its development and its administration to the research institute, providing funding for this purpose. In addition, the draft law provides for the withdrawal at the time of the commencement of the obligation to check prior to the examination of written diploma work using anti-plagiarism programmes. It is assumed that the examination of diploma work using the Single Anti-Plagiarism System will be carried out, starting from the academic year 2018/2019. The delay in applying this provision is due to the need to provide sufficient time for the development and implementation of the Single Anti-Plagic System. The Law on Higher Education requires university rectors to introduce their diploma work into the National Diploma Repository as soon as they pass their diploma exam. It also provides for the obligation for university rectors to submit data on their diploma work to the National Diploma Repository by 31 December 2016. The inclusion of diploma works in the National Diploma Repository was intended to extend the possibilities of comparison both by the promoter and by the Polish Accreditation Board. However, the deadline for fulfilling this obligation was too short in practice due to the wide variety of forms and formats in which the diploma work was submitted. In view of the numerous signals from the academic community, the project proposes to extend the implementation of this obligation until 31 December 2018. The draft provides for the entry into force of the new provisions on the day following the date of publication. It is desirable to introduce both the provisions for the development of the Single Anti-Plagiarism System as soon as possible and the provision for the extension of the deadline for the transfer to the repository of diplomatic works whose defence was successful after 30 September 2009. The project has financial implications and provides for public expenditure. The programme, which will cost up to PLN 14 million, will be financed from public funds, including mainly from the European Union's funds under the Operational Programme - Knowledge, education and development. Following the first reading and a positive examination of the proposed changes, the Committee on Education, Science and Youth asks the High Sejm to adopt the draft law submitted. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u132	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice with regard to the parliamentary draft law amending the Law on Higher Education and the Law amending the Law on Higher Education and certain other laws. The draft amends the Law of 27 July 2005 and the Act of 11 July 2014 amending the Law on Higher Education and certain other laws, hereinafter referred to as the Amending Act. The proposed amendments aim to create a Single Anti-Plagiarity System and introduce an obligation for universities to check written diploma work using this system. The project mainly responds to the need to harmonise the anti-Plagiarism system as a tool to address the problem of lack of integrity in the preparation of diploma work. The existing legal status has created an obligation on the part of the university and has thus imposed considerable costs on it to purchase and operate the relevant software. The proposed project addresses the difficulties of universities so far resulting from the creation and administration of significant costs associated with the purchase and administration of an appropriate anti-plagiarism system. Its aim is to increase university access to the system. The proposed amendment goes hand in hand with the principle of equal access to a solution for all universities regardless of their size or status. The introduction of the Single Anti-Plagiarism System, which will be made available free of charge to all universities, will provide comprehensive anti-Plagiarism services in the higher education system. By doing so, universities will be provided with a tool from the State to fulfil their obligation under the Law on Higher Education. The project foresees that the creation of the Single Anti-Plagic System will be the responsibility of the Minister responsible for higher education, who will be able to commission both its development and the administration of the research institute, while providing financial resources for this purpose. The solution proposed in the draft amendment of the law also extends the deadline for the implementation of the obligation for university rectors to provide written data on diploma work to the national repository, whose defence was successfully completed after 30 September 2009, from 31 December 2016 to 31 December 2017. In addition, the bill provides for the withdrawal at the time of the start of the obligation to check the diploma work using the anti-plagiarism system. The implementation of this obligation to use the single system will start from the academic year 2018/2019. The draft also provides for the entry into force of the new provisions 14 days after the date of publication. It is desirable to introduce both the provisions for the development of the Single Anti-Plagiarism System as soon as possible and the provision for the extension of the deadline for the transfer to the repository of diplomatic works whose defence was successful after 30 September 2009. The position of the Law and Justice Club is to adopt this bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Note that not only diploma, but also doctoral or scientific work are often part of the plagiarism. This is true even for large books. Therefore, in order for the university to be able to be responsible for its employees and to avoid plagiarism, it should be able to use such a system and even require that it benefit to a much wider extent than only in relation to its diploma work. On the other hand, the essential point is that we believe that limiting this order to research institutes alone is not justified. The amendment was tabled at the moment. As a matter of principle, it would be possible to consider, because the justification for this is a little lacking, we know what the costs of this new project will be, but a little short of the aggregate costs, how much it costs to operate anti-plagiar systems in all universities in the country at the moment, i.e. to compare current costs to future costs. We know that this would cost around 14 million, we know that there is a question of European Union funding, but we do not know the current costs and we cannot compare these two figures in any way. And, as I say, there is a market in which operators already operate, and this law actually deprives them of the opportunity to operate on the existing rules. So, to be honest, with a number of doubts, and taking into account the amendment, we need to decide what we are doing with this bill. Thank you. ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u139	Thank you very much. Mr Krystian Jarubas, the club of the Polish People's Party, will now speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u140	Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! The Way of Youth gathered at the Gallery! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I am pleased to present my position on the report of the committee on the draft law amending the Law on Higher Education and the Act amending the Law on Higher Education and some other laws, print Nos 433 and 504. Ladies and gentlemen! By proceeding with the above draft law, it would be appropriate to start with the definition of the word "plagiarism." Not every violation of the right to authorship is equivalent to a plagiarism, i.e. consciously, intentionally imputing the authorship to someone else's intellectual good. It may be due to acts that are innocent or committed by involuntary means. The diversity of situations can therefore affect criminal, civil and administrative liability, as well as ethical assessments of proceedings. At the same time, today we are dealing with many types of anti-Plagiarism systems. Some of them use simple methods of analysis of texts. It is most commonly used to compare texts using a one-to-one method, where a computer program scans works in search of identical fragments. But, ladies and gentlemen, just change the order of words in a sentence or insert microspaces or white sign - e.g. we combine two words with the symbol of the keyboard, written in white font - and the plagiarism will remain undetected. Therefore, the proposed amendments are so important. The amendments concern the creation of a Single Anti-Plagiarity System and the introduction of an obligation for universities to check written diploma work using this system and to extend the deadline for the performance of the obligation for university rectors to transfer to a nationwide repository of written diploma work, data on the diploma work which the defence took place after 30 September 2009. The existing state of law creates an obligation on the part of the university, at the same time imposing significant costs on the purchase and operation of the relevant software. It is therefore justified to develop a Single Anti-Plagic System, which is made available free of charge to all universities in the country. As a result, higher education institutions will be provided with a tool from the State to fulfil their obligation under the Law on Higher Education. The Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party will vote in favour of the proposed changes. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u178	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I am very pleased with this debate on the Single Anti-Plagiarism System. Thank you very much for all the votes and I am pleased that there is actually a high level of compatibility that we should fight plagiarism, even if it is not the agreement that we should use advanced online, electronic or IT instruments to do this. We even agree that it would be best if there was a Single Anti-Plagiarism System. The question of who has a Single Anti-Plagic System to do and who to pay for it remains to be discussed. The situation is this way. Allow me not to address all the questions one at a time, but I will try to accumulate it a bit, because the threads are repeating, so I will answer the next threads that appeared in the questions. The first point is that the Single Anti-Plagic System will be financed mostly from European funds and not from national funds. He asked why it would cost as much as 14 million PLN. In fact, the Member gave himself an answer right away. The program or Emp@tia system was supposed to cost 50 million, so 14 at 50 million is less. Basically, it's about spending less money on this system. One of your fellow Members pointed to a competition organized by the National Centre for Research and Development, where we were to spend PLN 100 million on the operation of the current anti-Plagiarism system, of which the list of beneficiaries consisted of 50 entities which were supposed to receive PLN 36 million. Thus, the cost proposed by the Ministry of Science and Higher Education is radically lower than the costs that are necessary to bear in the current system of anti-plagiarism programmes. The Member of the Civic Platform spoke about the fact that this system costs only PLN 2 million today. This system costs only that today because it is used by only half of the university, and they only check these works optionally, so only a small radius of work is checked. At the same time, she pointed out, a little maliciously comparing us to Slovakia... However, we should respect our neighbors, perhaps even learn from them. Since they have a problem with the quality of science, they use the best ways to improve this quality of science, and in this respect the Single Anti-Plagic System is one of the most innovative solutions. As far as other countries are concerned, this is indeed a European trend. So far this market has been distracted, but countries see the problem. What is it? I will refer again to what she said, pointing to Great Britain. In the UK, yes, there was a competitive anti-Plagiarism market. What's the end of it? The fact that after a few years the cost of handling anti-Plagiarism has increased by 40% and today the British are considering building something closer to the Single Anti-Plagic System, not responding to requirements, attention, not to the market, but to the intermediary, because we are dealing with a very specific situation in the case of the Single Anti-Plagic System. Namely, on one side we have an ORPD, i.e. the National Diploma Repository, which is managed by the Ministry of Science and Higher Education, the repository is in its jurisdiction, although it is directly managed by the Information Processing Centre. On the other hand, we have universities, which are also mostly public entities, which are supported by state subsidies, or we have private universities where students pay for their studies. The free market proposal is: let's put private entrepreneurs between these two public institutions, who will charge a fee for mediation in the exchange of data. It's not economically rational, it's not technologically rational, it's not rational under any circumstances. This is merely the introduction of a mandatory toll, which someone will collect, between two elements of the public higher education system, because private schools, even if they are private entities, are nevertheless part of the public system. The Polish State should make every effort to ensure that the higher education system, in general the education system, has as little financial burden as possible on both the state and private students who pay for their university studies. Thus, it will never happen that a system which is exclusively financed and maintained by the State is more expensive than a system in which there is an intermediary, because that means that this intermediary would have to add to its business. And no commercial entity has any interest in adding to the maintenance of the anti-plagiarism system. So what do the estimates look like - because there was also a question of how much it would cost - if you left this state as it is now? It is estimated that by multiplying the average number of diploma work that is defended at Polish universities, by the current offer, which was made by anti-Plagiarist companies present on the market, we can say that the annual cost of handling such a system is about 12 We are building, spending 14 million at once, but we will have a system which will cost approximately 2 million, which is going to cost us 10 million every year in the following years, so after a year the whole system will return to us, and in the following years it will be only profit. And this is not a good situation in which we will only go to the market. We are free marketers, we are in favour of private entities, but we are in favour of business where free entrepreneurs They're looking for private clients. There must be no free entrepreneurs who seek only public customers and want to operate under the public system. This is not a positive phenomenon for the economy. This system is therefore cheaper and more profitable financially for both private and public universities. Attention, it provides the same quality product to everyone, regardless of wealth, i.e. large universities, large universities from big cities, as well as very small universities, which are in smaller towns with less financial potential. So we have - here was the question of equality between these universities - ensured equality of all actors. This system is cheaper and this system is also more efficient in operation. I will refer here to the comments that were related to the question about the institute, why one institute. It was actually a difficult time. Since we have the nature of free marketers, at first we thought that we should make a maximum open invitation to tender, in which all the players present on the market who already have anti-plagiarism solutions or are working on anti-plagiar solutions, or would just like to come up with anti-plagiarism solutions. It's just that there's a risk. First of all, you know exactly what an IT tender means. He usually costs twice as much as originally planned, and most often ends in failure. Secondly, tenders tend to last for a long time, and in the end, tenders do not usually end in success. Why aren't they successful? It is because they do not clearly identify an entity with competence and are most often guided, as if for the safety of officials, only by the price criterion. We'd like to eliminate this paradigm. We think that our priority is to deliver the most functional system in the shortest possible time. If there is an institute in state resources that has the power to build such a system, we have no reason to look for another contractor, because today the State bears the cost of maintaining this institute, developing its potential. Few of this, this institute already has, we would say, part of the anti-Plagiarism system, because, as I said earlier, it is the host of a nationwide repository. If we wanted to commercialise this repository, it would mean that we would either have to make the repository an owner of another entity, that is, ordering universities to send and build a repository in some other university, in some other commercial entity, or we would have to build complex and cost-intensive IT instruments that would enable a large number of operators on the market to access this ORPD. It's purely illogical, it's impractical, it's illogical, it's nothing unreasonable. If there is an institute that can build an ORPD that can manage all data that is related to higher education and science, because the Information Processing Centre has and manages POL-on and OSF systems, i.e. systems that support funding streams of science, it has competence, knowledge, experience and what is most important, is proven. Believe it or not, it works. This is the part of computer science in Poland that works. So if it works, why not strengthen these competences and allow this institute to use a public contract or a public order? This is the reason why we would like to ask the High House to reject these two amendments, which only expose us to a long-term, costly and most likely completed failure of the tendering procedure which will not benefit either the State or the universities, or, most importantly, private entrepreneurs themselves, who are likely to protest for a long time and we will not have an anti-Plagiarism system. I don't know if I've answered all the questions. Ladies and gentlemen, if they wish, they will expect some additions, we are, of course, able to send them in writing. Once again, thank you very much for the debate. We share the opinion that yes, we want to fight plagiarism with anti-plagiarism systems. Thank you for the support of the Single Anti-Plagiarism System and we ask you to understand when it comes to selecting this single institute. At the end, of course, a philosophical note. You asked - it was also one of the threads - what will happen when this plagiarism will happen. So click, enter, and suddenly it turns out it's in red. I hope that the system will be functional and so simple that it can be done so quickly. Of course, the anti-Plagiarism system is a supporting instrument. The statement of plagiarism, or the result of the search in the anti-plagiarism, is not a decision. It's up to the promoter. And here is the absolute agreement, another agreement that connects us, that we cannot build an effective system that will support the honest behaviour of students, unless they are actively involved in this work promoters - I know something about it because I am a promoter myself and I know that it is not so easy and it costs a lot of time. The best anti-Plagiarism system is the promoter involved. But even an engaged promoter doesn't have a head in which he would have a whole base of 400,000. diploma work and 600 million fajli, which are collected on the Polish Internet, so he needs supporting instruments. The Single Anti-Plagiarism System is something that helps solve the problem, but which of itself will not solve the problem. As regards the comments that we should treat students fairly and fairly, i.e. If we're checking students, let's also check the academics - as a scientist I can answer that I am, of course, in favour, but this is not a technical issue that is related to this project. If you, ladies and gentlemen wish to make such changes, you may take such an initiative. This is a matter that is a matter for discussion. ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u182	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The draft law on amending the Act on Special Solutions related to the organisation of the North Atlantic Treaty Organisation Summit in the Republic of Poland in Warsaw in 2016 was drafted by the Council of Ministers due to the need to strengthen the security of NATO Summit participants and to preserve the appropriate security conditions after the end of this event. It is expected that the NATO Summit will be attended by Heads of State and Government from 28 countries, and there will be a significant number of accredited journalists as well as delegation participants. Due to the involvement of the Republic of Poland in international relations, given also the large possibilities of movement and geographical location and accessibility, Poland can be a convenient place to prepare and take actions aimed also at citizens and institutions of other countries. With a view to increasing the terrorist threat within the European Union, in order to ensure security during and immediately after the summit, the Council of Ministers proposed the following amendments to this law: Create a legal basis for the Government Protection Office to record during the NATO Summit, using technical means, image and sound events. Registered images or audio not containing evidence of the offence will be destroyed no later than 30 days after their registration. There will be protocol from the destruction. Creating the possibility of extending the service of police officers, ABW, State Fire Department, Border Guard, Government Protection Office and professional soldiers. The extension of service time is due to the need to ensure security for NATO participants and to ensure public order, which will require an increased number of uniformed officers on duty. In exchange for an extended period of service, it is assumed that officers and soldiers will be paid a flat-rate equivalent. Such regulation will help to maintain security at the end of the NATO Summit because, having received this equivalent, officers and soldiers will not use overtime free days. The ban on participation in spontaneous gatherings in Warsaw's capital city during the NATO summit, i.e. from the beginning of 7 July 2016 until 10 July 2016, only 4 days will therefore apply. The ban on attending these assemblies will only be an accident. This is intended to ensure an adequate level of security for the participants of the summit. Covering the implementation of public procurement tasks related to the organisation of the NATO Summit for the control of the Central Anti-corruption Office. In addition, it is proposed that the head of the CBA submit a report on this control by 30 September to the Sejm Committee on National Defence. This solution aims to strengthen the instruments to protect citizens' confidence in the integrity of state institutions organising the NATO Summit. In view of the solution proposed in the draft law, it should be assumed that the proposed regulation will undoubtedly contribute to strengthening the security of the NATO Summit participants and to ensuring more effective public order during and after this event. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u190	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! According to the draft amendment during the NATO Summit, officials of the Government Protection Office will have the right to record images and sound during the events related to this summit. The recorded material not containing evidence of the offence shall be destroyed no later than 30 days after the date of registration. A protocol for the destruction procedure will be drawn up. In addition, during the NATO Summit in Warsaw, participation in spontaneous assemblies will be prohibited. In view of the importance of the NATO Summit and the need to ensure the highest level of security for its participants, it will be possible to extend the time of service to police officers, the Internal Security Agency, the State Fire Department, the Border Guard and the Government Security Office. The project also provides for the possibility of extending the service of professional soldiers. According to the project, a flat-rate cash equivalent will be paid to officers and soldiers for overtime. This solution is intended to prevent group picking up of days off in overtime, which could weaken staff services and, as a result, adversely affect the safety of citizens. The draft amendment also states that the Central Anti-corruption Office will control orders related to the organisation of the NATO Summit in Warsaw. The Polish People's Party firmly believes that the provisions of this bill will in no way restrict citizens' freedom. ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u204	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The bill which the Senate presented to the High House is the implementation of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 18 November 2014 published in the Official Journal of 26 November 2014. It concerns the amendment of two laws: the Family Benefits Act and the Law on determining and paying welfare benefits, print No 370. The Constitutional Tribunal found it inappropriate to refuse the right to provide care to a disabled child's parent who resigns from employment or other work in connection with the need to take care of the child in a situation where the other parent has a fixed right to provide care for another disabled child in the family. The Constitutional Tribunal considered that such proceedings were incompatible with the Article. Article 71 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 sentence 2 of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. The proposal proposed by the High House to amend these laws removes this disadvantage. Two amendments were adopted by the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs on this project. The first one introduces a transitional provision whereby procedures for determining the parent's right to care services not completed before the date of entry into force of that law will be decided under the amended law, provided that the High Chamber so decides. The second amendment amends the date of entry into force of the Act on 1 January 2017. The Act has a financial impact of about PLN 30 million, hence it would be difficult to implement it in the current year because this amount was not provided for in the Budget Act. The Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy invites the High House to adopt the bill presented. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u205	Thank you very much, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of the clubs. I'm opening a discussion. Mrs Elżbieta Duda, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.

In accordance with the Article. 16a. 8 point 4 of the Family Benefits Act shall not be entitled to a special care allowance if the family member of the caretaker is entitled to an allowance to the family allowance referred to in Article. 10 the Act of 4 April 2014 on the establishment and payment of welfare benefits. Given the Court's judgment and the existence of conflicting provisions, it can be noted that this may lead to inconsistencies in law enforcement. It is therefore also proposed to put in place a clear provision to resolve this issue. ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u207	Thank you very much, Member. Mr Bożena Henczyc will now speak on behalf of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u208	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, On behalf of the Civic Platform Club, I would like to present our position on amending the Family Benefits Act and the Law on determining and paying benefits for carers, printers 370 and 499. The proposed law amending the Family Benefits Act and the Law establishing and paying welfare benefits is intended to bring the system of law into line with the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 18 November 2014 declaring the non-compliance of the article. Article 17 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 Point 5 (4) of the Law of 28 November 2003 on family benefits in force until 13 October 2011. The judgment is published in the Journal of Laws item. 1652. The Court held that this Article, as it stood until 13 October 2011, in so far as it precludes the granting of a right to provide care to a parent who resigns from employment or other employment in connection with the need to take care of a disabled child, where the second parent has a fixed right to provide care for another child in the family, is incompatible with Article. Article 71 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1nd sentence of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. According to art. 17, as examined by the Constitutional Tribunal, did not benefit from care if a person in the family had a fixed right to an allowance to the family allowance. According to that article, as examined by the Constitutional Tribunal, care was not granted if a family member had a fixed right to the allowance referred to in Article. 10 laws, either to provide care for this or for another child in the family. This provision expressed one of the negative reasons for obtaining State financial support in the event of resignation from employment or other employment or in the absence of professional activity due to the exercise of custody of a person legitimizing a disability decision, including indications of the need for permanent or long-term care or assistance of another person in connection with a significantly limited possibility of self-existence and the need for permanent participation of the child's guardian in the process of treatment, rehabilitation and education, or of a person legitimizing a judgment of a significant degree of disability on a daily basis. The most important result of the entry into force of the proposed law will be the alignment of the legal situation with the indications of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal and thus the fulfilment of the standard derived from the Article. Article 71 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 Second sentence of the Constitution. Our position, the position of the Civic Platform, is that we are in favour of the changes in this area, but we cannot agree with the fact that the Act would only enter into force from January 2017. The Civic Platform Club believes that this change should apply from 1 July 2017, because the financial consequences for the State do not exceed the budget possibilities to any extent. It is estimated that this amount will be less than 30 million per year, so it is really about 15 million per year. Since the government was able to find many millions for medical chambers and 27 million for Rydzyk's father, we believe that this 15 million should be found without a problem, even from the budget reserve. [[Why didn't you admit it last year?]] The Civic Platform Club therefore submits this amendment. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[You could've said it last year.]]

I present to the High House a report by the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs on the work on the government draft law on posting of workers in the framework of the provision of services, print No 408, which aims to implement European Union law. The draft of this law entered the Sejm on 1 April this year. At its first reading at the 17th meeting of the Sejm on 28 April, the High Chamber addressed it to the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs. May 11th this year During the work, the committee accepted both the amendments tabled by the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy and legislative amendments of an editorial nature. Among other things, in the article. 3 the term "working day' is added in Article. Paragraph 7 1 is replaced by the word "executor," in Article. 37 words from the entrusted entity were replaced by the words from the entrusted contractor in Article. Article 12 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 4 words 7 days have been replaced by "10 days" and the information requested by the State Labour Inspection has been extended in Article. Article 17 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 point (1) (a) and the word "address' has been replaced by the words "employer of the posting of a worker from the territory of the Republic of Poland' and "Article'. Article 18 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 Point 2 specifies that the transfer of the application for the execution of an administrative penalty or administrative fine to the head of the tax office may not take place earlier than 30 days after the service of the employer to the posting of a staff member from the territory of the Republic of Poland. The introduction of this regulation is justified because the employer may voluntarily pay the amount in question upon receipt of notification from the State Labour Inspection of the application for enforcement of the penalty or fine. In such a case, it would be pointless to initiate enforcement proceedings, which would then have to be terminated, and would have been unnecessary to bear the costs involved. Leaving the employer's obligation 30 days for voluntary payment of charges seems to be a sufficient period. On expiry of that period, it may be assumed that there will be no voluntary settlement of duties, and enforcement proceedings are therefore necessary, which are intended to transfer the application to the head of the tax office. The current text of the article. 27 shall be indicated as the mouth. 1 and paragraph 1 is added. 2 as follows: The same penalty shall apply to an employer posting an employee to the territory of the Republic of Poland or to a person acting on his behalf if the State Labour Inspection finds that the worker concerned cannot be regarded as a posted worker within the territory of the Republic of Poland. Recital 11 of the Implementation Directive shows that Member States should ensure that provisions are in place to protect workers who are not actually posted in a sufficient way. Although the recitals of the directive are not normative and serve merely to interpret its provisions, it is appropriate to introduce a standard in the proposed law which will indirectly protect workers who are not actually posted. The threat of fines for employers' actions to de facto reduce the costs of their employees' work in the territory of another Member State should be preventive to those employers and should prevent their use of posting institutions. Art. 30 is replaced by the following: In the Law of 17 November 1964, the Civil Procedure Code in Article. 1103 § 2 is replaced by the following: Cases from an employee's claim concerning the provision of conditions of employment in accordance with the Posting of Workers Act in the framework of the provision of services belong also to the national jurisdiction where the employee is either posted to work in the territory of the Republic of Poland by an employer established in a Member State of the European Union. The consequence of this amendment is the amendment in Article. Thirty-six proposed law. The amendment seeks to waive the possibility proposed in the proposed act of bringing labour inspectors to the labour courts to determine what law is applicable to the relationship between the worker and his employer if the inspector finds that such a worker cannot be regarded as a posted worker. This provision is proposed to be abandoned because there would be serious doubts in the interpretation and application of this provision by labour courts. The Supreme Court drew attention to the above in its opinion to the proposed law. Art. 33 - is another amendment - is replaced by the following: The Law of 26 June 1974. The Labour Code is amended as follows: Link No 1 repeals point 19 and Chapter IIa of Chapter 2 is repealed. Since the proposed law introduces an amendment to the Labour Code consisting in repealing in Section 2 of that Chapter IIa Code concerning the conditions of employment of workers assigned to work in the territory of the Republic of Poland from a Member State of the European Union, it is also necessary to repeal in reference No 1 to the Labour Code point 19. It concerns Directive 96/71/EC of 16 December 1996 concerning the posting of workers in the framework of the provision of services. After the article. 33 it is decided to add Article. 33a as follows: The Law of 21 November 1996 on museums is amended as follows: 30 The following Article shall be inserted: 30a as follows: access to information to ensure the safety of museums on grounds of fire risk protection, theft and other risks that threaten destruction or loss of harvests shall be restricted, and Chapter 4 shall be repealed. 31a.
