Art. 18 of this project was supplemented by a regulation concerning the date of transmission of the application for the execution of an administrative penalty or administrative fine by the State Labour Inspection to the head of the tax office - no earlier than 30 days after the date of service of the employer to the posting of a worker from the territory of the Republic of Poland. The amendment is important, as in fact the employer is left to the posting of a worker from the territory of the Republic of Poland the time for voluntary payment of duties. Without this amendment, a request for enforcement could in practice be sent to the head of the tax office after voluntary payment by the employer, which would unnecessarily generate costs both on the part of the State Labour Inspection and on the part of the enforcement authority. Art. 27 projects concerning criminal liability for non-compliance with certain provisions of the Act have been supplemented by the possibility of imposing a fine on the employer of the posting of a worker on the territory of the Republic of Poland or on a person acting on his behalf if the State Labour Inspection finds that the worker concerned cannot be regarded as a posted worker on the territory of the Republic of Poland. Amendments to the other provisions of the draft law are either a consequence of the above amendments or are of a legislative or linguistic nature. ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u229	Thank you very much. Mr Stefan Romecki now has the floor on behalf of the Kukiz Club15. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u230	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Club I have the honour - this is a great honour - to present a position on the government proposal for the Posting of Workers Act in the framework of the provision of services. The proposed law aims to implement the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 15 May 2014. The previous regulations were in Section 2 of Chapter IIa of the Labour Code: Conditions of employment of workers assigned to work in the territory of the Republic of Poland from a Member State of the European Union, as well as in the Act on State Labour Inspection. Directive 96/71 of the European Parliament and of the Council, known as the Basic Directive, regulates situations where workers have been temporarily posted by their employer to another Member State in the framework of services provided in the State. According to Art. Directive 2014/67 requires Member States to adopt, by 18 June 2016, the laws and administrative provisions necessary to comply with it. As we read in the explanatory memorandum to the draft law, this directive, the implementation of which is to be ensured by this draft law, is primarily aimed at ensuring compliance with an adequate level of protection of the rights of posted workers in order to provide services across borders. This includes, in particular, the enforcement of minimum employment conditions applicable in the Member State where the service is to be provided, while facilitating the exercise of the freedom to provide services by service providers and promoting fair competition between service providers and thereby supporting the functioning of the internal market. Since the proposed law consequently implies both the inclusion in a single legal act of the provisions implementing the Basic Directive and the transfer of them from the laws in force and the establishment of new regulations implementing Directive 2014/67, the Poselski Kukiz Club15 raises no objections to the proposed project. Thank you.

If the bill aimed at improving the protection of workers is only criticised by employers' organisations, it can be said that there is a balance in nature. However, if criticism is accompanied by the State Labour Inspection, and lawyers are multiplying the interpretation doubts that can impact on workers' rights, then we should start worrying. There is no doubt and there can be no doubt that Poland will implement into its legal system the objectives of Directive 2014/67 of the European Parliament and of the Council of Europe - pacta sunt servanda - but we must, as a matter of urgency, bend over a number of problems which have not been taken into account during the work of the committee, as the law adopted in such a form will be another legal buffet. However, we hope that the implementing rules will clarify these issues. Since the beginning of the draft law, numerous reservations have been raised about the text of the law and some of these issues still need to be properly regulated. They concerned and still concern, among others. problems in defining the terms of temporary referral, posted worker and posting employer, and the problem of joint and several liability, which has been defined in detail for a narrow category of work, while not ensuring this protection to other workers. Modernly, such protection should cover all workers. The proposed law will have negative consequences for Polish employers providing services on the European Union market and their employees, because the draft law does not safeguard the interests of Polish companies posting workers to work abroad from the situations of imposing and enforcing penalties or fines on them, and these penalties are primarily disproportionate and discriminatory. At the same time, too many obligations are imposed on employers, including disproportionate to the fines. Despite taking into account the comments of employers' organisations, there are still too short deadlines for replying to PIP applications, although, as my predecessors suggested here, extending the deadline to 10 days is already much better than the 7 days proposed in its original form. However, we still believe that the concepts I mentioned earlier should be clarified. The law regulates the posting of workers to the territory of the Republic of Poland, while Polish companies posting workers abroad fear this directive because of the increase in costs, e.g. a typical company employing and sending nannies to Germany employs them for a contract order, and the subject matter of the order is defined so that part of the work is carried out in Poland. The reason for this is that from such contracts the contribution is paid to the Polish and not to the German ZUS, because the contribution paid to this German is much higher. The issue of regulating this scope, these reservations, is very important because it is our employees who are most vulnerable to abuse. This is not only due to their quantity, it should be remembered that a significant proportion of the workers posted to provide services abroad use temporary employment agencies, and in their case there is really something to fight for. For them, ensuring conditions that are no worse than those recognised as a fair minimum on the host market may determine the economic viability of the entire trip. In extreme cases for them and for their families it can be an existential issue. According to the Provincial practice, the group of posted workers consists mainly of middle-level workers and management staff. It is clear that most of these problems do not concern them at all, so it is important to be aware of the objectives pursued by individual countries when concluding a compromise on Directive 2014/67 and in the design of the Directive itself as an act of secondary law of the European Union. The directive points to the desired state of affairs and, although the modern one agrees with the general direction of the directive, we are looking for answers to the question whether a project which raises so many doubts can ensure that these objectives are effectively achieved. Let us remember that developed Western economies protect their markets in this directive. We want to protect our citizens. We must not allow the status quo to be maintained, nor must we tolerate permanent multiannual delegations and the actual inability to claim workers' claims, particularly when they return to their home country. We cannot agree to the helplessness of state authorities towards foreign employers who do not comply with regulations. We need to translate this political compromise into the language of the law in order to ensure that posted workers are protected and that employers do not put more than a reasonable and necessary measure. Any legislative error will result in proceedings before labour courts, which today are still bursting at seams. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Although the deadline is being followed, the rush is a terrible advisor here, but we hope that all these doubts will be clarified by means of regulations so that the Modern will support this project. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Therefore, Member States have been obliged to adopt internal regulations in their legal order to ensure that such workers have a minimum level of employment conditions in their territory. The proposed law also includes rules on access to information about posting of workers to Poland, as well as declarations by the company posting employees. There are also specific sanctions for infringements, fines for 1 000. Up to 30,000. zł. Polish employers who post workers to other countries and fail to comply with their obligations under the directive and legislation will also pay penalties. The delegate shall provide him with, among other things, a staff member. Conditions of employment which are no less favourable than those laid down in the national labour code of the standard of working time, daily and weekly rest and rest. The draft law is a continuation and extension of the Energy Efficiency Act of 15 April 2011. Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 25 October 2012 on energy efficiency requires the implementation of these principles in the Polish legal system. This Directive obliges the Member States of the Union to achieve the objective of increasing energy efficiency by 20% by the end of 2020. The provisions of the Act of 15 April 2011 have been extended until the end of 2016 by the Act of 29 April 2015 amending the Energy Efficiency Act. We proceed with the examination of item 9 of the agenda: Report from the Infrastructure Commission on the draft law on investments in wind power plants (prints 315 and 365). I ask Mr Grzegorz Schreiber to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u294	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Committee on Infrastructure to submit a report on the Parliamentary draft law on investments in wind power plants. The report is contained in print No 365. The aim of the project is to determine the conditions, rules and modalities for the location, implementation and operation of wind power plants and the conditions for the location of residential buildings in the vicinity of wind power plants. There are currently no rules in Polish legislation that explicitly regulate the location of wind power plants, which results in numerous protests of residents living in neighbouring areas with existing or planned wind power plants. These conflicts are due to the potential negative impact of these investments on human health and life, livestock and wild animals, as these are also subject to this risk. The most important phenomena associated with the operation of the power plant include noise and infrasound emissions and the so-called effect. Shadow flickering. In addition, there are concerns about the technical state of wind power plants as a whole and their individual equipment, including technical equipment. In extreme cases of technical negligence by the owner, this can lead and unfortunately leads to failure and dissection of the shovels. All these problems were highlighted by the Supreme Audit Chamber in its information on the results of the audit. This is included in the information on the location and construction of wind farms on land, hereinafter referred to as "the information', dated 26 July 2015. The information negatively addressed the Supreme Audit Chamber to the process of location and subsequent use of wind power plants. In response to this, a group of applicants prepared a project that was examined by the Infrastructure Commission. The regulations proposed in the draft law relate to the following issues. There are, of course, new legal provisions, such as the introduction of the definition of wind power plants, technical elements of wind power plants, the establishment that the location of wind power plants with a capacity of more than 40 kW is based solely on the local spatial planning plan, the determination of the distance within which wind power plants can be located from residential buildings and certain forms of nature protection, equal to or more than 10 times the height of the wind power plant measured from the ground level to the highest point of the building, including technical elements, in particular the rotor with the shovels, the local plan providing for the location of wind power plants, which is to contain the maximum total height of the wind power plant and is to be drawn up at least for an area where the residential power plant cannot be located and its limit is the maximum height of the wind power plant in it. The draft, which we are discussing in the committee's submission, also sets out the rules and procedures of the Technical Supervision Authority when assessing the technical condition of the wind power plant. Other changes - because these are the new provisions that this law introduces, but this law also introduces amendments to other laws, e.g. to the Construction Law Act. Here we have a change in the definition of the structure by removing the wind power plant from the current content, indicating the voivodeship as a higher degree body of architectural and construction administration in relation to the old town and the first degree body in matters of construction works and works for the wind power plant. With regard to the Land Planning and Land Planning Act, the principle is that a local plan providing for the location of buildings should enable the location of micro-installations within the meaning of the Renewable Energy Sources Act to be located also in the case of a non-production site, unless the establishment of a local plan prohibits the location of such facilities. With regard to the Act on the provision of environmental information and its protection and public participation in environmental protection, there is a change in the designation of the Regional Environment Director as the authority responsible for the decision on environmental conditions. Finally, as regards transitional provisions, the draft law proposes regulations relating to administrative proceedings initiated prior to the entry into force of the Act on draft local plans and studies of conditions and spatial planning of municipalities, as well as the conditions for obtaining decisions of the Technical Supervision Authority authorising the operation of wind power plants. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Parliamentary draft law on investment in wind power plants entered the Sejm on 19 February 2016. On 10 March, it was sent to the Committee on Infrastructure for First Reading. It took place on March 16th. The Commission has set up an emergency subcommittee to which it has addressed the proposal. The subcommittee adopted a report at three meetings on 16, 17 and 18 March, which in turn came to committee. The Commission adopted the report on 30 March. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Many amendments were adopted in the course of the work on this project, both in the subcommittee and in the committee. This must be said clearly and clearly: this project aroused widespread public interest, on the one hand, expressed through applause and clear approval for its procession, for its creation, and on the other hand, it gathered critical opinions. This has also been reflected in the work of the committee. The Commission worked on an open courthouse, invited social parties who participated in committee meetings and expressed their views, just as I mentioned earlier - some of them expressing approval for many of the provisions of this project, others very critically referring to them. It must therefore be made very clear that a number of amendments to this proposal were adopted during the work of the committee and the subcommittee. It is not as it could be read in some places that the project was adopted without any changes as presented by the applicants. A dozen amendments were accepted in the subcommittee, and they were adopted at the committee meeting on the basis of 13 amendments. Minority requests have been made, and I will tell the High House as soon as possible. With regard to the amendments which the subcommittee introduced - of course there were a number of editorial amendments - it is certainly worth recalling at this point the amendment in the article. The 28th change led to one thing. The project originally divided into proceedings concerning permits for the construction of residential buildings and wind farms. The project divided these situations into three subgroups. The subcommittee has decided to harmonise this situation. On the one hand, in relation to building permits for residential buildings and wind power plants, it introduced a provision which states that all of them will be carried out on the basis of the existing rules, provided that the distance requirements were maintained in the planning plan, i.e. uniformed the procedure. Those groups that were in the draft applicants were changed at that time. This seems to be a very good change. A number of changes, I think I will not prolong my speech, subcommittees, very important changes, were also adopted at the meeting of the Infrastructure Committee, which I mentioned, which adopted the subcommittee report. As I said, 13 changes have been made and I will focus, and I will recall one amendment to the article. 30, it's worth a brief description here. There are changes in the article. 30 led to legal regulations that allow housing to be implemented, provided that there are local plans. It was so important, it is so important that it actually raised doubts about the inhabitants who, on the one hand, were punished by the builders of the power plant, and then were to be punished with additional punishment, they could not expand their homes. We took the first step in committee, allowing, through the adoption of amendments to the Article. 30... At the same time we have announced, and this, I think, will be fulfilled by my club, further moving changes that will go somewhat beyond the local plan, but will also make changes in the so-called. Wookies, which should also be adapted and should enable the building of residential homes in those areas which are subject to a windmill risk, although this will be decided by the municipality which will take decisions in this respect in accordance with the will of the inhabitants. Ladies and gentlemen! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I think so much for such a general introduction to our discussion. Of course, it is also my duty to say that a number of amendments have been tabled during the work of the Committee on Infrastructure, which have not been accepted by the committee. Some of them were reported as minority proposals and such minority proposals, the committee noted 20. However, after the committee worked through this project, the committee believes that it will contribute to the regulation of the rules of the location of wind power plants in Poland - local plan, distance criterion, construction process through permission for use, decision allowing operation and subsequent operation. In addition, the demands are being met, which I think is quite important, the Supreme Audit Chamber, which was included in the information, which the committee at the committee meeting last week, such an additional committee meeting discussing the NIK reports, listened to, took note of it. [[Applause]] [[Chairman of the proceedings includes Deputy Marshal Stanisław Tyszka of the Sejm]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u295	Thank you, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of the clubs. I'm opening a discussion. Mrs Anna Paluch, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u296	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! As the rapporteur pointed out a few moments ago, the process of the location of wind power plants creates a lot of social conflicts, a huge social resonance, which is why the law we are debating is of great interest. Due to the thinness of time, I will have to discuss very briefly and briefly the changes that are there and the amendments that my club is reporting at second reading. Article 1 of the proposal, specifying the scope of the Act, provides that the Act does not apply to investments carried out in the maritime areas of the Republic of Poland. So any whining that the law will hinder the development of wind energy is a lie. I would like to remind you that for years in the concept of spatial development of the country there has been shown an area of coastal sea, where wind farms can be located without putting people living nearby at risk of nuisance. I will point out that the provisions of the Act do not apply to devices with low power, not more than the power of micro-installations within the meaning of Article. 2 point 19 of the Renewable Energy Act. That is exactly what the article contains. 23 of the draft discussed here, contained in the committee's report in print No 365. I will recall the report of the Supreme Audit Chamber of 2014 on procedures for the location and construction of wind power plants. It contains e.g. information that about 1/3 of the location of these investments is on land belonging to persons who perform important functions in the authorities of local municipalities: wójts, mayors and councillors. The last report of the Supreme Audit Chamber, dated April 2016, by No 47/2016/D/15/502/KIN, concludes that, in order to avoid a conflict of interests of persons acting in the commune bodies, these persons submitted statements of conflict of interest at the start of planning processes and those who did not disable themselves were subject to disciplinary sanctions. You can see that this is a very important problem, and the Supreme Auditorium also sees it. This draft law contained in print No 365 responds to these problems identified in the reports of the Supreme Audit Chamber. It states that, as Art. 3 - the location of the wind power plant is based solely on the local spatial planning plan. This is, let me remind you, the procedure described in detail in the Spatial Planning and Development Act, supported by expert opinions and requiring public consultation. High Izbo, in order to avoid the negative impact of wind power plants on settlement areas, the Act sets the minimum distance between a wind power plant and a residential building or a mixed-function building consisting of a residential function. This is 10 times the total amount of the power plant measured from ground level to its highest point. This also applies to the location of new residential buildings near wind farms. However, this is not a restriction on reconstruction, reconstruction, expansion, renovation, assembly or reconstruction of existing residential buildings. The local plan for the location of the wind power plant should set its maximum overall height. So every now and then the technicians have to check his condition, but this cost is, compared to the investment, neglected. Although it is different in different parts of Italy, it is adopted up to 2 km in terms of regulations in this country. The same is true in Scotland - 2 km, in the Czech Republic - up to 1 km, say up to 800 m. In Denmark, which is often referred to in various debates, there is also a multiple of the total wind power plant measured from the foundation to the highest point. This, too, has been the charge that this is what we have decided to measure, but this is generally measured in Europe, from the foundation to the highest point. Today the world is very dynamic, especially the geopolitical situation is changing rapidly. Poland must think about how at every step it strengthens the intellectual potential of the Polish army. Therefore, the establishment of the Academy of War Art consists of providing the Polish army with modern strategic studies, a specific teaching and research offer in the field of social sciences related to the security and defence of the state. The Academy of War Art will play an important role in the system of preparing and improving professional leadership, staff and logistics forces of all levels of command: tactical, operational and strategic. The Academy of War Art will improve the qualifications of officers of all types of Armed Forces in post-graduate studies, qualification and improvement courses. The procedure introduced in the bill for creating and conducting forms of professional improvement will allow for a quick response to changing educational needs of the Armed Forces not only in quantitative but also in programme terms, thus contributing to the increase of the teaching offer of the Academy. ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u465	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! I am pleased on behalf of the Council of Ministers to present a draft law on the rules for determining the remuneration of persons running certain companies. Ladies and gentlemen, today's legal status is that it is so-called. Chimney bill. It is a law which, in the legal sense, is, to put it mildly, obsolete. We have diagnosed, and it was not necessary to carry out any in-depth audits here, that this is a normative act, which is largely avoided, not used, giving birth to pathology. Therefore, the law, the draft law which I present, abolishes the chimney law in the sense that it significantly restricts its application. This law, which I am now talking about, will apply to companies. Ladies and gentlemen, the law is primarily addressed to corporate bodies, i.e. entities that represent, exercise rights from shares, shares in companies controlled by the State Treasury, as well as companies controlled by the local government. Its aim is to introduce uniform, transparent and fair rules for the remuneration of managers of these companies, as well as members of supervisory boards. The Act is in line with the principles, with best corporate governance practices, strengthens good practices in the operation of supervision in commercial companies. It also appears that the law refers to market-based rules on wage formation, ensures transparency, but also refers to a certain sense of social justice in the sense that it introduces certain restrictions as regards the remuneration of members of the boards of commercial companies. With regard to the very way in which the remuneration of the members of the Management Board is determined, the main arrangements laid down by the Act are primarily the flexible formation of the basic remuneration. This remuneration will depend, in accordance with the elementary logic, on the size of the company's income, employment and value of the assets. Attention should also be paid to the incentive part of the remuneration. In principle, the remuneration will be divided into a fixed part, a variable part, and unlike the Chimney Act this bonus part will be significantly higher than until now, because it will not amount to around 20%, but in principle up to 50%, and in the case of some companies - up to 100%. This is the answer to the problem that really concerns what we pay for. We do not want to pay for functions, we want to pay first and foremost for real performance, which will be set up by supervisory boards. We anticipate that the basic formula for employment will be a civil law agreement, while at the same time we want to eliminate these, let's face it, pathological bonuses associated with the departures of the presidents. Thus, the maximum period of termination of the contract will be 3 months, the right to check-in will be acquired after 12 months of office. It will not be more than 3 times the fixed monthly salary. Finally, we want to introduce restrictions on compensation for the prohibition of competition. The right to prohibit competition will only be acquired after 3 months. This prohibition of competition will not be paid for more than 6 months. As a general rule, we want to introduce such a rule so that the ban on competition is actually... so that the ban on competition is economical. The point is that there have been far-reaching abuses here, where the bans on competition were received by members of boards of companies that did not have any competition. We also refer to the so-called. missionary companies when determining so-called. The NIP, namely in the case of missionary companies, is such a possibility that the settlement of the results is primarily about missions, public tasks, and not solely about financial effects. It is also intended to eliminate the pathology of additional earnings in capital groups. We do not rule out functions in companies belonging to the capital group, but such sitting should be free of charge. In conclusion, a word about the very construction of the bill. By creating a bill, we assumed that the chimney law actually creates a separate regime of company law for certain companies. The advantage of this project is, in fact, to remain in the mainstream of company law, corporate law, because the law primarily refers to, so to speak, does not affect the consistency of the Commercial Companies Code. This is its great advantage, hence the obligation that we impose is not an obligation imposed on companies, but on authorities exercising rights from shares, shares. Thank you very much.

There's only a bunch of trees, which means the mansion remains. But there is something in the church in this village: two large paintings of Witold Pilecki's hand. The company leases agricultural property of the Agricultural Property Stock State Treasury with a total area of ca. 684 ha. They are located in the municipalities of Kamiennik and Otmuchów in the district of Nysa. They oppose the conclusion of further contracts with a 100% foreign capital-based tenant, Agrohop and its related entities. ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u513	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! June 19th this year In Nowogrod near Łomża for the 23rd time will be organized by the Regional Cultural Centre in Łomża National Days of Kurpiowska Culture. The culture of the inhabitants of the Kurpie Green Forest is one of the richest and most faithful preserved yet poorly known regional cultures of our country. As part of the National Days of Kurpiowska Culture, for 23 years there has been a Regional Review of Kapels, Śpiewaków and People's Reptiles in Zbójna, the National Competition of Kurpiowski's Teams in Nowogrod, workshops of dying competitions, non-competition performances of folk and folklore bands, among which, to our joy, there are more and more such ones that place Kurpiowski's songs and dances in their repertoires, and in their authentic form. This is proof that excavating from oblivion and cultivating archaic, it would seem, traditions make sense. The opportunity to participate in the National Days of Kurpiowska Culture leads their participants, getting younger, to remember their grandmothers and grandparents in order to be able to present another song or talk. This makes an unforced, authentic relationship between generations, and it greatly enriches and extends the archive to unfamiliar works, and this is also what organizers of the National Days of Kurpiowska Culture are concerned about. In addition, during the course of the review, the audience will have the opportunity to know through their personal participation artistic and utility works, blacksmithing, cutting, etc. Because of the rapidly changing world, old customs and traditions are disappearing. Civilizational progress forces us to replace some professions with others, and we have to accept that. However, we cannot accept and we cannot allow knowledge of our traditions, customs and regional cultures to disappear. By means of events such as the National Days of Kurpie Culture, we have the chance to restore them and preserve them, which is why the overarching goal of the National Days of Kurpie Culture is to preserve the tradition and convey the folk cultural heritage and to promote and present folk art effectively by promoting the customs and ceremonies of Kurpie culture, traditional music, singing and games. Organisation 23. National Days of Kurpiowska Culture significantly contributes to strengthening the position of Kurpiowszczyzna on the cultural map of Poland. The experts emphasize that this is one of the last regions in Poland, in which elements of folk culture are preserved so well. The event also influences the formation of a positive image of the Podlaskie voivodship by building and consolidating the cultural identity of the region. Over the years of the event, the number of youngest participants in the event is constantly increasing, which is not terrified of learning and making lyrics in the face of their grandmothers and grandparents. On the contrary, they find joy in discovering the fun that the context gives them with authentic, not Cepelian folklore. The ability to say something in a glow, to woven on authentic grandmother's looms, to bring to a school handcuffed at a blacksmith's ornament is no longer a charm, but a reason to be proud. In the last Sunday, over 150 folk artists appeared in the Robotic Regional Review of Kapels, Singers and People's Reptiles in front of the audience, and young people and the elderly played together on the stage, where the age difference was often over 50 years. This shows that organizers are very often implementing a program of cultivating and popularizing the tradition of Kurpie folklore. Small children appear next to experienced older performers. This is the best sign and hope that the Kurpie traditions will not disappear. From this point of view, I would like to thank all those involved professionally and socially, and in particular the Municipal Cultural Centre in Zbójna, Regional Cultural Centre in Łomża, for working on the organisation of the National Days of Culture in Kurpiowska, popularization of knowledge about the region and taking care of the culture of this region of Poland. We all know the words of the Polish ethnographic artist Adam Chętnik, for whom the Kurpie land was home. The words that were his life's credo: Doing so, I wonder if it would be good for Poland. Adam the Chotnik also said, "Whoever loves his heart [[♪ The bell ♪]] The corner, this country will also love the whole country. In addition to this corner beloved by this outstanding activist, museum and researcher of Kurpie culture, I would like to invite everyone to 23. National Days of Kurpiowska Culture 19 June in Nowogrod. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-18-sejm-19-1.u517	Statement on the 67th anniversary of Anatoly Radzivonik's death. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On 12 May, we celebrated the 67th anniversary of the death of Anatoly Radzivonik, alias Olech, the lieutenant of the Polish Army, one of the commanders of troops of the anti-communist underground and independence, the last commander of organized structures of the Polish underground of independence on New Zealand. The figures in today's Poland almost unknown, and in Belarus, due to the actions of the Soviet propaganda apparatus, very negatively evaluated. Anatol Radzivonik was born in Briansk, Russia. His parents were evacuated there from Volkowska by the Tsarski authorities. After the Polish-Soviet war, however, the Radziwoniks returned to their hometowns, and Anatol began his studies at the State Male Teacher Seminary in Slonim. After graduating from school he worked in a rural school, teaching Polish and Belarusian, while participating in the creation of local scouting structures. Radziwonik was appointed to the Polish Army in the summer of 1938. The war found him in Iszczołnani, where he lived after having been released from the army. We don't know exactly when he joined the independence conspiracy. However, in 1943 he was already in charge of one of the underground facilities of the AK Szchuczyn Circuit, codenamed "Łąka." During his service in the Home Army, he was promoted to officer rank - lieutenant. From the winter of 1944, after moving to the guerrilla, he commanded the 3rd platoon of the 2nd company of 7th Battalion 77. The infantry regiment of the AK, which was led by the Lieutenants. Bojomira Trzórianski.Stocie and por. Jan Piwnik, Ponure. Anatol Radzivonik participated in numerous fights against German occupation forces. On April 15, 1944, a patrol separated from his platoon disarmed the German police station and the Belarusian police in Możejkowie Mały. During the same month his troops also crashed the German crew in Możejkow Wielki and then German troops in Kowczyk. In June Radzivonik took part in the liquidation of the German garrison in Jewish. In July, Olech headed for Vilnius to take part in the action of the Ostrzy Gate. His squad was late, so he wasn't disarmed. The commander therefore led the subordinates to Szchuczyn, where he continued his underground activities. Due to the difficult contact with the structures of the AK in the spring of 1945, a 4967 circuit was established, over which Olech took command. Anatol Radzivonik became the main enemy of the NKVD in 1945. His squad was counting. then about 800 people. The NKVD has long tried to eliminate all the off-road facilities. Polish resistance was only broken after 4 years. He died on 12 May 1949, near the village of Raczkowszczyzna, while trying to break through the Soviet manhunt. A few years ago, on the 64th anniversary of the death of Radzivonik, a memorial cross was unveiled at the place where he died. They were sentenced to high fines for their prayers: the President of the Polish Union in Belarus and the President of the Polish Army Association in Belarus. The cross was removed by unknown perpetrators 2 months after the sacrifice, but returned to its place in December 2013. It was supplemented by a stone and a plaque. Hello and praise the heroes! Thank you.
